. Then
H=E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(R,J)
where

J=\begin{cases}Rz&\text{if }[\alpha]\sim A_{1},\\
Rz+R{\bar{z}}&\text{if }[\alpha]\sim A_{1}^{2},\\
Rz+R{\bar{z}}+R{\bar{\bar{z}}}&\text{if }[\alpha]\sim A_{1}^{3},\\
Rz_{1}+R{\bar{z_{1}}}+R(z_{2}-\bar{z_{2}})&\text{if }[\alpha]\sim A_{2}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Since
x_{[\alpha]}(z)\in E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(R,J)
, by Proposition
5.2.1
, we have
H\subset E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(R,J)
. For the reverse inclusion, we need to prove that
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in J_{[\beta]}
and
[\beta]\in\Phi_{\rho}
. Observe that there exist a sequence of roots
[\alpha_{i}]\ (i=1,\dots,n)
such that
[\alpha_{1}]=[\alpha],[\alpha_{n}]=[\beta]
, and for every
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
, the pair of roots
[\alpha_{i}],[\alpha_{i+1}]
contained in some connected subsystem of
\Phi_{\rho}
of rank
2
. Now, by applying Lemma
5.5.5
(below) recursively to the pairs
([\alpha_{i}],[\alpha_{i+1}])
, we obtain the desired result.
∎

Lemma 5.5.5
.

Let the notation be as established in Proposition
5.5.4
. Suppose
\Phi^{\prime}
is a connected subsystem of
\Phi_{\rho}
with rank 2. If
[\gamma]\in\Phi^{\prime}
and
s\in R
such that
x_{[\gamma]}(s)\in H
, then
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
[\beta]\in\Phi^{\prime}
and
t\in I_{[\beta]}
, where
I
is an
\theta
-invariant ideal defined similarly to the ideal
J
in Proposition
5.5.4
by replacing
z
with
s
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
denote the angle between
[\beta]
and
[\gamma]
. For
r\in R
, let
r^{\prime}
denote either
r,\bar{r}
or
\bar{\bar{r}}
. By
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
, we mean the first and second coordinate of
r=(r_{1},r_{2})\in\mathcal{A}(R)
.
Consider the following table outlining the possible types of choices for
\Phi^{\prime}
:

Type

\Phi_{\rho}

Type of Roots

Possible Choices of
\Phi^{\prime}

Long

Short

{}^{2}A_{3}

C_{2}(=B_{2}
)

A_{1}^{2}

Case A.
\Phi^{\prime}\sim A_{2}
which contains roots of the type
A_{1}
.
This case arises only when
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}D_{n}\ (n\geq 4),{}^{3}D_{4}
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
. Let us consider the following subcases:

(A1)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
\mu=\pi/3
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of type
(b-i)
. Here
[\gamma],[\beta]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
are of type
A_{1}
. For
r\in R_{\theta}
, we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r)]=x_{[\beta]}(rs)\in H.

(A2)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
\mu
is arbitrary.
Observe that, we can find a sequence
[\gamma_{1}],\dots,[\gamma_{m}]
of roots in
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
2\leq m\leq 6,[\gamma_{1}]=[\gamma],[\gamma_{m}]=[\beta]
and the angle between
[\gamma_{i}]
and
[\gamma_{i+1}]
is
\pi/3
for every
i=1,\dots,m-1
. Then, by applying Case (A1) to the pair of roots
([\gamma_{i-1}],[\gamma_{i}])
, we have
x_{[\gamma_{i}]}(rs)\in H
for every
r\in R_{\theta}
and
i=2,\dots,m
. In particular, by Lemma
5.5.2
,
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
, as desired.

Case B.
\Phi^{\prime}\sim A_{2}
which contains roots of the type
A_{1}^{2}
.
This case arises only when
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1}\ (n\geq 3){}^{2}A_{2n}\ (n\geq 3)
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
. Let us consider the following subcases:

(B1)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
\mu=\pi/3
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(b-ii)
. Here
[\gamma],[\beta]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
are of type
A_{1}^{2}
. For
r\in R
, we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r^{\prime})]=x_{[\beta]}(rs^{\prime})\in H.

(B2)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
\mu
is arbitrary.
Observe that, we can find a sequence
[\gamma_{1}],\dots,[\gamma_{m}]
of roots in
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
2\leq m\leq 6,[\gamma_{1}]=[\gamma],[\gamma_{m}]=[\beta]
and the angle between
[\gamma_{i}]
and
[\gamma_{i+1}]
is
\pi/3
for every
i=1,\dots,m-1
. Then, by applying Case (B1) to the pair of roots
([\gamma_{i-1}],[\gamma_{i}])
, we have
x_{[\gamma_{i}]}(rs^{\prime})\in H
for every
r\in R
and
i=2,\dots,m
. In particular,
x_{[\beta]}(rs^{\prime})\in H
for every
r\in R
.

Note that, we can find a subsystem
\Phi^{\prime\prime}
of
\Phi_{\rho}
of type
B_{2}
such that
[\beta]\in\Phi^{\prime\prime}
. Since
x_{[\beta]}(rs^{\prime})\in H
, by Case C (if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1}
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
) and by Case D (if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n}
) (see below), we have
x_{[\beta]}(\bar{r}\bar{s^{\prime}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. In particular,
x_{[\beta]}(rs)
and
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Therefore,
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
, as desired.

Case C.
\Phi^{\prime}\sim B_{2}
which contains roots of the type
A_{1}
and
A_{1}^{2}
.
This case arises only when
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1}\ (n\geq 2),{}^{2}D_{n}\ (n\geq 4)
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
. Let us consider the following subcases:

(C1)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
\mu=\pi/4
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(d-i)
. Here
[\gamma],2[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\beta],[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
. For
r\in R
, we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r/2)]=x_{[\beta]}(rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}s/4)\in H.

Now put
-r
instead of
r
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(-rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}s/4)\in H
. But then

x_{[\beta]}(rs)=\{x_{[\beta]}(rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}s/4)\}\{x_{[\beta]}(-rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}s/4)\}^{-1}\in H.

(C2)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
\mu=\pi/4
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(c-i)
. Here
[\gamma],[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}
. For
r\in R
, we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r)]=x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s}+\bar{r}s)\in H.

(C3)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
\mu=\pi/2
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(d-i)
. Here
[\gamma],[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]+[\gamma],[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
. For
r\in R_{\theta}
, we have

[x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r),x_{[\gamma]}(s)]=x_{[\beta]}(rs)x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm rs\bar{s})\in H.

Now, by Case (C2), we know that
x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm rs\bar{s})\in H
. Hence

x_{[\beta]}(rs)=\{x_{[\beta]}(rs)x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm rs\bar{s})\}\{x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm rs\bar{s})\}^{-1}\in H.

In particular,
x_{[\beta]}(s)\in H
. By Case (C1) and Case (C2), we have
x_{[\beta]}(s+\bar{s})\in H
. But then
x_{[\beta]}(\bar{s})\in H
. Now, by switching roles of
{[\beta]}
and
[\gamma]
, we get
x_{[\gamma]}(\bar{s})\in H
. If we replace
s
by
\bar{s}
in above argument, then we get
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s})\in H
, for every
r\in R_{\theta}
.

(C4)

[\gamma]
and
\mu
are arbitrary.
Observe that, we can find a sequence
[\gamma_{1}],\dots,[\gamma_{m}]
of roots in
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
2\leq m\leq 8,[\gamma_{1}]=[\gamma],[\gamma_{m}]=[\beta]
and the angle between
[\gamma_{i}]
and
[\gamma_{i+1}]
is
\pi/4
for every
i=1,\dots,m-1
.

(a)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}
then
m
must be odd. By applying Case (C1) to pairs
([\gamma_{i-1}],[\gamma_{i}])
, Case (C2) to pairs
([\gamma_{i}],[\gamma_{i+1}])
for
i=2,4,\dots,m-1
, inductively, we get
x_{[\beta]}((r+\bar{r})s)\in H
for every
r\in R
. Now by Lemma
5.5.2
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(b)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{2}
then
m
must be even. Note that
[\gamma_{m-1}]
is of type
A_{1}
. Hence, by the above case, we have
x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(s)\in H
. Now by applying Case (C1) to the pair
([\gamma_{m-1}],[\gamma_{m}])
, we conclude that
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(c)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}
then
m
must be even. By applying Case (C3) to pairs
([\gamma_{i}],[\gamma_{i+2}])
for
i=1,3,\dots,m-3
, inductively, we get
x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(s)\in H
. Now by applying Case (C2) to the pair
([\gamma_{m-1}],[\gamma_{m}])
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s}+\bar{r}s)\in H
for every
r\in R
. Finally by Lemma
5.5.2
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for all
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(d)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{2}
then
m
must be odd. By applying Case (C2) to pairs
([\gamma_{i-1}],[\gamma_{i}])
, Case (C1) to pairs
([\gamma_{i}],[\gamma_{i+1}])
for
i=2,4,\dots,m-1
, inductively, we get
x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}(r_{2}\bar{s}+\bar{r_{2}}s))\in H
for every
r_{1},r_{2}\in R
. Now by applying Case (C3) to pairs
([\gamma_{i},\gamma_{i+2}])
for
i=1,3,\dots,m-2,
inductively, we get
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}s)
and
x_{[\beta]}(r_{4}\bar{s})\in H
for every
r_{3},r_{4}\in R_{\theta}
.
But then for every
r_{5}\in R
, we have

x_{[\beta]}(r_{5}^{2}s)=x_{[\beta]}(r_{5}(r_{5}s+\bar{r_{5}}\bar{s}))\{x_{[\beta]}(r_{5}\bar{r_{5}}\bar{s})\}^{-1}\in H.

Similarly, we have
x_{[\beta]}(r_{6}^{2}\bar{s})\in H
for every
r_{6}\in R
.
Finally, for given
r\in R
we have

x_{[\beta]}(rs)=x_{[\beta]}\Big(\Big(\frac{1+r}{2}\Big)^{2}s\Big)\Big\{x_{[\beta]}\Big(\Big(\frac{1-r}{2}\Big)^{2}s\Big)\Big\}^{-1}\in H.

Similarly, we have
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s})\in H
for every
r\in R
. By Lemma
5.5.2
, we conclude that
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for all
t\in I_{[\beta]}.

Case D.
\Phi^{\prime}\sim B_{2}
which contains roots of the type
A_{1}^{2}
and
A_{2}
.
This case arises only when
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n}\ (n\geq 2)
. Let us consider the following subcases:

(D1)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
\mu=\pi/4
.
First observe that the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(d-ii)
. Here
[\gamma],2[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta],[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{2}
. In this case, for
r=(r_{1},r_{2})\in\mathcal{A}(R)
we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r_{1}^{\prime}/2,r_{2}^{\prime}/4)]=x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}s^{\prime}/2,r_{2}s\bar{s}/4)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r_{2}^{\prime}s/4)\in H.

By putting
-r_{1}
instead of
r_{1}
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(-r_{1}s^{\prime}/2,r_{2}s\bar{s}/4)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r_{2}^{\prime}s/4)\in H
. But then

\begin{split}x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}s^{\prime},\frac{r_{1}\bar{r_{1}}}{2}s\bar{s})&=x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}s^{\prime},\frac{r_{2}+\bar{r_{2}}}{2}s\bar{s})\\
&=\{x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}s^{\prime}/2,r_{2}s\bar{s}/4)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r_{2}s/4)\}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\{x_{[\beta]}(-r_{1}s^{\prime}/2,r_{2}s\bar{s}/4)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r_{2}s/4)\}^{-1}\in H.\end{split}

Now observe that the pair
[\gamma]
and
2[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of type
(a_{2}-ii)
. Here
[\gamma],[\delta]:=2[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]=1/2([\gamma]+[\delta])\sim A_{2}
. In this case, for
r\in R
we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\delta]}(\pm r)]=x_{[\beta]}(0,\bar{r}s-r\bar{s})\in H.

We now claim that for every
(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{4}s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}(Rs^{\prime})
, the element
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{4}s^{\prime})\in H
. As in proof of Lemma
5.5.1
, we consider the following decomposition of
(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{4}s^{\prime}):

(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{4}s^{\prime})=(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{3}\bar{r_{3}}s\bar{s}/2)\oplus(0,(r_{4}s^{\prime}-\overline{r_{4}s^{\prime}})/2).

Therefore, we have

x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{4}s^{\prime})=x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}s^{\prime},r_{3}\bar{r_{3}}s\bar{s}/2)x_{[\beta]}(0,(r_{4}s^{\prime}-\overline{r_{4}s^{\prime}})/2)\in H.

Finally, by Proposition
4.4.10
(or by Proposition
4.1
of
[
1
]
), we have

w_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(1)x_{[\gamma]}(s){w_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(1)}^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]}(-\bar{s}).

(5.2)

Therefore,
x_{[\gamma]}(-\bar{s})\in H
and hence
x_{[\gamma]}(\bar{s})\in H.
Replacing
s
by
\bar{s}
in above, we get
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}},r_{4}\bar{s^{\prime}})\in H
for every
(r_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}},r_{4}\bar{s^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{A}(R\bar{s^{\prime}})
. In particular, we have
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}s,r_{4}s)\in H
(resp.,
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}\bar{s},r_{4}\bar{s})\in H
) for every
(r_{3}s,r_{4}s)\in\mathcal{A}(Rs)
(resp.,
(r_{3}\bar{s},r_{4}\bar{s})\in\mathcal{A}(R\bar{s})
).

(D2)

[\gamma]\sim A_{2}
and
\mu=\pi/4
.
Note that, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(c-ii)
. Here
[\gamma],[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{2}
. For
r\in R
, we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s_{1},s_{2}),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r^{\prime},r\bar{r}/2)]=x_{[\beta]}(rs_{1}^{\prime})\in H.

(5.3)

By (
5.2
), we also have
x_{[\beta]}(\bar{r}\bar{s_{1}^{\prime}})\in H
. In particular, for every
r\in R
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(rs_{1})\in H
and
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s_{1}})\in H
.

Observe that, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-2[\gamma]
is of the type
(d-ii)
. Here
[\beta]-[\gamma],[\gamma]\sim A_{2}
and
[\beta],[\beta]-2[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
. For
r\in R
we have

[x_{[\beta]-2[\gamma]}(\pm r^{\prime}),x_{[\gamma]}(s_{1},s_{2})]=x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(rs_{1}^{\prime},r\bar{r}s_{2}^{\prime})x_{[\beta]}(\pm r^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime\prime})\in H.

(5.4)

Note that,
(x_{[\gamma]}(s_{1},s_{2}))^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]}(-s_{1},\bar{s_{2}})\in H
. By above, we have
x_{[\beta]}(s_{1})\in H
. By applying case (D1) to the pair
([\beta],[\gamma])
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(2s_{1},2s_{1}\bar{s_{1}})\in H
. But then

x_{[\gamma]}(s_{1},\bar{s_{2}})=x_{[\gamma]}(-s_{1},\bar{s_{2}})x_{[\gamma]}(2s_{1},2s_{1}\bar{s_{1}})\in H.

Put
(s_{1},\bar{s_{2}})
instead of
(s_{1},s_{2})
and
-r
instead of
r
in (
5.4
), we get

x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(-rs_{1}^{\prime},r\bar{r}\bar{s_{2}^{\prime}})x_{[\beta]}(\pm(-r^{\prime})\bar{s_{2}^{\prime\prime}})\in H.

Since the elements
x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\cdot)
and
x_{[\beta]}(\cdot)
are commutes with each other, we have

\displaystyle x_{[\beta]}(\pm r^{\prime}(s_{2}^{\prime\prime}-\bar{s_{2}^{\prime\prime}}))

\displaystyle=\{x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(rs_{1}^{\prime},r\bar{r}s_{2}^{\prime})x_{[\beta]}(\pm r^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime\prime})\}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\{x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(-rs_{1}^{\prime},r\bar{r}\bar{s_{2}^{\prime}})x_{[\beta]}(\pm(-r^{\prime})\bar{s_{2}^{\prime\prime}})\}\in H.

In particular, we have
x_{[\beta]}(\frac{r}{2}(s_{2}-\bar{s_{2}}))\in H
for every
r\in R
. Again by (
5.3
), we have
x_{[\beta]}(\frac{r}{2}(s_{1}\bar{s_{1}}))=x_{[\beta]}(\frac{r}{2}(s_{2}+\bar{s_{2}}))\in H
for every
r\in R
. But then

x_{[\beta]}(rs_{2})=x_{[\beta]}(\frac{r}{2}(s_{2}+\bar{s_{2}}))x_{[\beta]}(\frac{r}{2}(s_{2}-\bar{s_{2}}))\in H,

for every
r\in R
. Similarly, we have
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s_{2}})\in H
for every
r\in R
.

(D3)

[\gamma]
and
\mu
are arbitrary.
Observe that, we can find a sequence
[\gamma_{1}],\dots,[\gamma_{m}]
of roots in
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
2\leq m\leq 8,[\gamma_{1}]=[\gamma],[\gamma_{m}]=[\beta]
and the angle between
[\gamma_{i}]
and
[\gamma_{i+1}]
is
\pi/4
for every
i=1,\dots,m-1
.

(a)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{2}
then
m
must be odd. By applying Case (D1) to pairs
([\alpha_{i}],[\alpha_{i+1}])
and Case (D2) to pairs
([\alpha_{i+1}],[\alpha_{i+2}])
for every
i=1,3,\dots,m-2
, recursively, we get
x_{[\beta]}(rs)\in H
and
x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Therefore, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(b)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{2}
then
m
must be even. Note that
[\gamma_{m-1}]\sim A_{1}^{2}
, by the above case,
x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(s)\in H
and
x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(\bar{s})\in H
. By applying case (D1) to the pair
([\alpha_{m-1}],[\alpha_{m}])
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}s,r_{2}s)\in H
and
x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}\bar{s},r_{2}\bar{s})\in H
for every
(r_{1}s,r_{2}s)\in\mathcal{A}(Rs)
. Finally, by the proof of Lemma
5.5.3
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(c)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{2}
then
m
must be even. Note that
[\gamma_{2}]\sim A_{1}^{2}
, by applying case (D2) to the pair
([\gamma_{1}],[\gamma_{2}])
, we get
x_{[\gamma_{2}]}(rs_{1})
,
x_{[\gamma_{2}]}(r\bar{s_{1}})
,
x_{[\gamma_{2}]}(rs_{2})
,
x_{[\gamma_{2}]}(r\bar{s_{2}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Now by Case 1 above, we have
x_{[\beta]}(rs_{1}),

x_{[\beta]}(rs_{2}),

x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s_{1}}),

x_{[\beta]}(r\bar{s_{2}})\in H
for every
r\in R
.
Therefore, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(d)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{2}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{2}
then
m
must be odd. Note that
[\gamma_{m-1}]\sim A_{1}^{2}
, by the case 3 above, we have
x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(s_{1}),x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(s_{2}),x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(\bar{s_{1}}),x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(\bar{s_{2}})\in H
. By applying case (D1) to the pair
([\gamma_{m-1}],[\gamma_{m}])
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}s_{1},r_{2}s_{1})
,
x_{[\beta]}(r_{1}\bar{s_{1}},r_{2}\bar{s_{1}})\in H
for every
(r_{1}s_{1},r_{2}s_{1})\in\mathcal{A}(Rs_{1})
and
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}s_{2},r_{4}s_{2})
,
x_{[\beta]}(r_{3}\bar{s_{2}},r_{4}\bar{s_{2}})\in H
for every
(r_{3}s_{2},r_{4}s_{2})\in\mathcal{A}(Rs_{2})
. Therefore, by the proof of Lemma
5.5.3
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

Case E.
\Phi^{\prime}\sim G_{2}
which contains roots of the type
A_{1}
and
A_{1}^{3}
.
This case arises only when
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{3}D_{4}
. Let us consider the following subcases:

(E1)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
\mu=\pi/6
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of the type
(e)
. Here
[\gamma],3[\beta]-[\gamma],3[\beta]-2[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\beta],[\beta]-[\gamma],2[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3}
. For
r\in R
, we have

\displaystyle[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r/2)]=x_{[\beta]}(rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm rr^{\prime}s/4)

\displaystyle x_{3[\beta]-2[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s/8)x_{3[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s^{2}/8)\in H.

Note that, long roots in
G_{2}
form a subsystem of type
A_{2}
. Hence, by Case A, we have
x_{3[\beta]-2[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s/8)\in H
and
x_{3[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s^{2}/8)\in H
. But then

\displaystyle\{x_{[\beta]}(rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm rr^{\prime}s/4)x_{3[\beta]-2[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s/8)x_{3[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s^{2}/8)\}

\displaystyle\{x_{3[\beta]-2[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s/8)x_{3[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s^{2}/8)\}^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{[\beta]}(rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm rr^{\prime}s/4)\in H.

Now put
-r
instead of
r
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(-rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm rr^{\prime}s/4)\in H
. Finally,

x_{[\beta]}(rs)=\{x_{[\beta]}(rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm rr^{\prime}s/4)\}\{x_{[\beta]}(-rs/2)x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm rr^{\prime}s/4)\}^{-1}\in H.

(E2)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3}
and
\mu=\pi/6
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of type
(g)
. Here
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3},[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}
. For
r\in R
, we have

[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r^{\prime})]=x_{[\beta]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H.

(E3)

[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3}
and
\mu=\pi/3
.
In this case, the pair
[\gamma]
and
[\beta]-[\gamma]
is of type
(f)
. Here
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3},[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3},[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{3},2[\beta]-[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\gamma]+[\beta]\sim A_{1}
. For
r\in R
, we have

\displaystyle[x_{[\gamma]}(s),x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm r/2)]=x_{[\beta]}((r^{\prime}s\pm rs^{\prime})/2)x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm(r\bar{s}\bar{\bar{s}}+\bar{r}s\bar{\bar{s}}+\bar{\bar{r}}s\bar{s})/2)

\displaystyle x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm(r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{s}}+\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s+r\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{s})/2)\in H.

Here
(s^{\prime},r^{\prime})=(\bar{s},\bar{r})
or
(\bar{\bar{s}},\bar{\bar{r}})
.
Note that
x_{[\gamma]}(-s)=\{x_{[\gamma]}(s)\}^{-1}\in H
. Hence if we replace
r
(resp.,
s
) by
-r
(resp.,
-s
), then

\displaystyle x_{[\beta]}((r^{\prime}s\pm rs^{\prime})/2)x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm(-r\bar{s}\bar{\bar{s}}-\bar{r}s\bar{\bar{s}}-\bar{\bar{r}}s\bar{s})/2)

\displaystyle x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm(-r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{s}}-\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s-r\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{s})/2)\in H.

Since
x_{[\beta]}(\cdot),x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\cdot)
and
x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\cdot)
commutes with each other, we have

\displaystyle x_{[\beta]}(r^{\prime}s\pm rs^{\prime})=\{x_{[\beta]}((r^{\prime}s\pm rs^{\prime})/2)x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm(r\bar{s}\bar{\bar{s}}+\bar{r}s\bar{\bar{s}}+\bar{\bar{r}}s\bar{s})/2)

\displaystyle x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm(r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{s}}+\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s+r\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{s})/2)\}\{x_{2[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm(-r\bar{r}\bar{\bar{s}}-\bar{r}\bar{\bar{r}}s-r\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{s})/2)

\displaystyle x_{[\gamma]+[\beta]}(\pm(-r\bar{s}\bar{\bar{s}}-\bar{r}s\bar{\bar{s}}-\bar{\bar{r}}s\bar{s})/2)x_{[\beta]}((r^{\prime}s\pm rs^{\prime})/2)\}\in H.

(E4)

[\gamma]
and
\mu
are arbitrary.
Observe that, we can find a sequence
[\gamma_{1}],\dots,[\gamma_{m}]
of roots in
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
2\leq m\leq 12,[\gamma_{1}]=[\gamma],[\gamma_{m}]=[\beta]
and the angle between
[\gamma_{i}]
and
[\gamma_{i+1}]
is
\pi/6
for every
i=1,\dots,m-1
.

(a)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}
then
m
must be odd. By applying Case (E1) to pairs
([\gamma_{i}],[\gamma_{i+1}])
and Case (E2) to pairs
([\gamma_{i+1}],[\gamma_{i+2}])
for
i=1,3,\dots,m-2
, recursively, we get,
x_{[\beta]}((r+\bar{r}+\bar{\bar{r}})s)\in H
for every
r\in R
. Now by Lemma
5.5.2
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H,
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(b)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{3}
then
m
must be even. Since
[\gamma_{m-1}]\sim A_{1}
, by the above Case, we have
x_{[\gamma_{m-1}]}(s)\in H
. Now by applying Case (E1) to the pair
([\gamma_{m-1}],[\gamma_{m}])
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(rs)\in H
for every
r\in R
. Hence we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H,
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}=I
.

(c)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}
then
m
must be even. By apply Case (E2) to pairs
([\gamma_{i}],[\gamma_{i+1}])
and Case (E1) to pairs
([\gamma_{i+1}],[\gamma_{i+2}])
for
i=1,3,\dots,m-3
, recursively, and finally Case (E2) to the pair
([\gamma_{m-1}],[\gamma_{m}])
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Now by Lemma
5.5.2
, we have
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H,
for every
t\in I_{[\beta]}
.

(d)

If
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}^{3}
and
[\beta]\sim A_{1}^{3}
then
m
must be odd and
m\geq 3
. We first show that
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I
. Note that
[\gamma_{3}]\sim A_{1}^{3}
. By applying Case (E2) to the pair
([\gamma_{1}],[\gamma_{2}])
, we get
x_{[\gamma_{2}]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Next, by applying Case (E1) on the pair
([\gamma_{2}],[\gamma_{3}])
, we conclude that
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Finally, by applying Case (E3) on the pair
([\gamma_{1}],[\gamma_{3}])
, we obtain
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs\pm r^{\prime}s^{\prime})\in H
for every
r\in R
(where
a^{\prime}
denotes
\bar{a}
or
\bar{\bar{a}}
).

Suppose
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs+r^{\prime}s^{\prime})\in H
. Then
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(r^{\prime\prime}s^{\prime\prime})\in H
for every
r\in R
(where
a^{\prime\prime}=\bar{a}
if
a^{\prime}=\bar{\bar{a}}
and vice-versa). By reversing the roles of
[\gamma_{1}]
and
[\gamma_{3}]
together with roles of
s
and
s^{\prime\prime}
, we get
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
and
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(r^{\prime}s^{\prime})\in H
. Since
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(s)\in H
, it follows that
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(\bar{s})
and
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(\bar{\bar{s}})
are also in
H
. Applying the same process again with
\bar{s}
(resp.,
\bar{\bar{s}}
), we obtain
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(r\bar{s}^{\prime\prime})\in H
(resp.,
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(r\bar{\bar{s}}^{\prime\prime})\in H
) for every
r\in R
. In particular, we have
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\gamma_{3}]}
.

Now, suppose
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs-r^{\prime}s^{\prime})\in H
. Then
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(2rs+r^{\prime\prime}s^{\prime\prime})\in H
for every
r\in R
. By reversing the roles of
[\gamma_{1}]
and
[\gamma_{3}]
together with roles of
s
and
2s+s^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}((2r+r^{\prime})s+(2\bar{r}+\bar{r^{\prime}})\bar{s}+(2\bar{\bar{r}}+\bar{\bar{r^{\prime}}})\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
and
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(4s+4s^{\prime\prime}+s^{\prime})\in H
. Since the map
r\mapstochar\rightarrow 2r+r^{\prime}
from
R
to itself is surjective, we have
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Consequently,
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(3s^{\prime})\in H
, and hence
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(\bar{s})
and
x_{[\gamma_{1}]}(\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
(as
1/3\in R
). Applying the same process again with
\bar{s}
and
\bar{\bar{s}}
, we get
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(3rs)=x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs+\bar{r}\bar{s}+\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs-\bar{r}\bar{s})x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs-\bar{\bar{r}}\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. Thus, we get
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(rs)\in H
for every
r\in R
. Similarly, we can show that
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(r\bar{s})\in H
and
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(r\bar{\bar{s}})\in H
for every
r\in R
. In particular, we have
x_{[\gamma_{3}]}(t)\in H
for every
t\in I_{[\gamma_{3}]}
, as required.

Now if
m=3
, then we are done. If not, we repeat this process for the pair
([\gamma_{i}],[\gamma_{i+2}])
for every
i=3,\dots,m-2
to obtain desired result.

This completes the proof of our lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.4.1
.
Let
J
be as in the hypothesis of Proposition
5.4.1
. Let
t,u\in J
. Then there exists
[\alpha],[\beta]\in\Phi_{\rho}
such that
t\in J_{[\alpha]}(H)
and
u\in J_{[\beta]}(H)
. Let
[\gamma]\in\Phi_{\rho}
be such that it is either of type
A_{1}^{2}
or
A_{1}^{3}
. By Proposition
5.5.4
, for every
r\in R
we have
x_{[\gamma]}(rt),x_{[\gamma]}(\bar{t}),x_{[\gamma]}(u)\in H
and hence
x_{[\gamma]}(t+u)\in H
. Therefore, if
t,u\in J
and
r\in R
then we have
t+u,rt,\bar{t}\in J
. Thus
J
is a
\theta
-invariant ideal of
R
. Now for the second assertion, it follows from Proposition
5.5.4
that
E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(J)\subset H
. Since
H
is normalized by
E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(R)
, we conclude that
E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(R,J)\subset H
, as desired. ∎

5.6
Proof of Proposition
5.4.2

Let
\Phi_{\rho}
be an irreducible root system. We fix a simple system
\Delta_{\rho}=\{[\alpha_{1}],\dots,[\alpha_{l}]\}
of
\Phi_{\rho}
. Recall that, for a root
[\alpha]=\sum_{i=1}^{l}m_{i}[\alpha_{i}]\in\Phi_{\rho}
, we defined
ht([\alpha])=\sum_{i=1}^{l}m_{i}
. We say a root
[\beta]
is
highest
if the height of
[\beta]
is maximal, i.e.,
ht([\beta])=\max\{ht([\alpha])\mid[\alpha]\in\Phi_{\rho}\}
. Note that there is a unique highest root in an irreducible root system and it is a long positive root.
Therefore we sometimes call it
highest long root
. Similarly, we say
[\gamma]
is a
highest short root
if
ht([\gamma])=\max\{ht([\alpha])\mid[\alpha]\in\Phi_{\rho}\text{ and }[\alpha]\text{ is short root}\}
. There is a unique highest short root in an irreducible root system.

Lemma 5.6.1
.

Let
x:=\prod_{[\alpha]\in\Phi_{\rho}^{+}}x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})\in U(R)\cap H
with
t_{[\alpha]}\in R_{[\alpha]}
(the product is taken over disjoint roots in any fixed order). Then
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})\in H
for all
[\alpha]\in\Phi_{\rho}^{+}
.

Proof.

The proof closely resembles that of Lemma 3.1 and Proposition 1 in Section 3 of
[
3
]
. However, the calculations presented here are distinct from those in
[
3
]
. For the convenience of the reader, we provide the full proof below.

Case A.
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{3}
:
In this case, after the twist,
\Phi_{\rho}
becomes a root system of type
B_{2}
. Let
[\alpha]
and
[\beta]
be the simple roots, with
[\alpha]
being the long root. We first claim that

\textit{if }x=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in H,\textit{ then }x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t),x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in H.

(5.5)

For any
r\in R_{[\alpha]}=R_{\theta}
, we have

H\ni[x_{-[\alpha]}(r),x]=x_{[\beta]}(\pm rt)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm rt\bar{t}).

Since
x\in H
then so is
x^{-1}=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(-t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(-u)
. Therefore we can replacing
t
and
u
by
-t
and
-u
, respectively, and we get
x_{[\beta]}(\pm r(-t))x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm rt\bar{t})\in H
. But then

x_{[\beta]}(\pm 2rt)=\{x_{[\beta]}(\pm rt)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm rt\bar{t})\}\{x_{[\beta]}(\pm r(-t))x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm rt\bar{t})\}^{-1}\in H.

Put
r=\pm 1/2
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
. By Proposition
5.5.4
, we get
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)\in H
and hence
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in H
. This proves (
5.5
).
Now let
x=x_{[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(v)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(w)\in H
. Then

[x_{[\alpha]}(1),x]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm t\bar{t})\in H.

By (
5.5
), we have
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t)\in H
. Again by Proposition
5.5.4
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(t)\in H
. Consequently,
x_{1}:=x_{[\alpha]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(v)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(w)\in H
. Now,

[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{1}]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm u)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm u\pm(v+\bar{v}))\in H.

Again, by (
5.5
),
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm u)\in H
, and hence
x_{[\alpha]}(u)\in H
(by Proposition
5.5.4
). But then

x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(v)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(w)\in H.

Finally, by (
5.5
),
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(v)\in H
and
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(w)\in H
, as desired.

Case B.
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{4}
:
In this case, after the twist,
\Phi_{\rho}
becomes a root system of type
B_{2}
. Let
[\alpha]
and
[\beta]
be the simple roots, with
[\alpha]
being the long root. We first claim that

\textit{if }x=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in H,\textit{ then }x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t),x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in H.

(5.6)

For any
r=(r_{1},r_{2})\in R_{[\alpha]}=\mathcal{A}(R)
, we have

H\ni[x_{[\beta]}(r),x]=x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{1}t^{\prime}_{1}),

(5.7)

where
r^{\prime}_{1}
denotes either
r_{1}
or
\bar{r_{1}}
, similar for
t^{\prime}_{1}
.
Now

H\ni[x_{-[\beta]}(r),x]=x_{[\alpha]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{1}t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\alpha]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{2}u)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{1}u^{\prime},r^{\prime}_{2}u\bar{u}).

By (
5.7
) and Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\alpha]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{1}t^{\prime}_{1})\in H
. But then

x_{[\alpha]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{2}u)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm r^{\prime}_{1}u^{\prime},r^{\prime}_{2}u\bar{u})\in H.

Now if we put
(r_{1},r_{2})=(1,1/2)
, then we get
x_{[\alpha]}(\pm u/2)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm u^{\prime},u\bar{u}/2)\in H
and if we put
(r_{1},r_{2})=(-1,1/2)
, then we get
x_{[\alpha]}(\pm u/2)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm(-u^{\prime}),u\bar{u}/2)\in H
. But then

x_{[\alpha]}(\pm u)=\{x_{[\alpha]}(\pm u/2)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm u^{\prime},u\bar{u}/2)\}\{x_{[\alpha]}(\pm u/2)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm(-u^{\prime}),u\bar{u}/2)\}\in H.

Again by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in H
and hence
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)\in H
. This proves (
5.6
).

Now let
x=x_{[\beta]}(s)x_{[\alpha]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(v)\in H
. Then

[x_{[\alpha]}(1),x]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2})x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(0,\pm(v-\bar{v}))x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm s_{2})\in H,

where
s=(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathcal{A}(R)
and
s_{1}^{\prime}=s_{1}
or
\bar{s_{1}}
, similar for
s^{\prime}_{2}
.
By (
5.6
), we have

x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2}\pm(v-\bar{v}))\in H\text{ and }x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm s_{2})\in H.

Again by Proposition
5.5.4
, we get
x_{[\beta]}(s)
. But then
x_{1}:=x_{[\alpha]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(v)\in H
.
Now,

[x_{[\beta]}(1,1/2),x_{1}]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t^{\prime},t\bar{t}/2\pm(t\bar{u_{1}}-\bar{t}u_{1}))x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm t\pm u^{\prime}_{1})\in H.

Again by (
5.6
),
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t^{\prime},t\bar{t}/2\pm(t\bar{u_{1}}-\bar{t}u_{1}))\in H
and hence
x_{[\alpha]}(t)\in H
(by Proposition
5.5.4
). But then
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(v)\in H
and again by (
5.6
),
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(u)\in H
and
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(v)\in H
, as desired.

Case C.
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{3}D_{4}
:
In this case, after the twist,
\Phi_{\rho}
becomes a root system of type
G_{2}
. Let
[\alpha]
and
[\beta]
be the simple roots, with
[\alpha]
being the long root.
We first claim that if

x=x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(u)x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v)\in H,

then
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t),x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(u),x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v)\in H.

Note that
[x_{[\alpha]}(1),x]=x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm u)\in H
and
[x_{-[\alpha]}(1),x]=x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm v)\in H
. But then, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v)\in H
,
x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(u)\in H
and hence
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t)\in H
, which proves the claim.
We next claim that if

x=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v)x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(w)\in H,

then
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t),x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u),x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v),x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(w)\in H
. For any
r\in R_{\theta}
, we have

y(r)=[x_{-[\alpha]}(r),x]=x_{[\beta]}(\pm rt)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm rtt^{\prime})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm r^{2}t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}}\pm rw)x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm rt\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}})\in H,

where
t^{\prime}=\bar{t}
or
\bar{\bar{t}}
. For any
s\in R_{\theta}
, we have

\displaystyle y(r,s)

\displaystyle=[x_{[\alpha]}(s),y(r)]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm srt)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm sr^{2}tt^{\prime})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm sr^{3}t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}})

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464ptx_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm s^{2}r^{3}t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}}\pm sr^{2}t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}}\pm srw)\in H.

Let
x_{1}:=y(r,s)^{-1}y(1,rs)=x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm s(r^{2}-r)tt^{\prime})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v^{\prime})x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(u^{\prime})\in H
. By above we have
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm s(r^{2}-r)tt^{\prime})\in H
. Put
r=-1
and
s=1/2
, we have
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(tt^{\prime})\in H
.
But then, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm tt^{\prime}),x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}}),x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}})\in H
. Hence,

y(1)x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}})^{-1}x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm t\bar{t}\bar{\bar{t}})^{-1}x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm tt^{\prime})^{-1}=x_{[\beta]}(\pm t)x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm w)\in H.

Further,
[x_{-2[\alpha]-3[\beta]}(1),x_{[\beta]}(\pm t)x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm w)]=x_{-[\alpha]}(\pm w)\in H
. By Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm w)\in H
and hence
x_{[\beta]}(\pm t)\in H
. Again by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)\in H
and hence
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v)x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(w)\in H
. By above claim, we have
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)
,
x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(v)
,
x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(w)\in H
, as desired.

Finally, let
x=x_{[\beta]}(t_{1})x_{[\alpha]}(t_{2})x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t_{3})x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t_{4})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{5})x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{6})\in H
. Note that

[x_{[\alpha]}(1),x]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t_{1})x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm t_{1}t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm t_{1}\bar{t}_{1}\bar{\bar{t}}_{1})x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(\pm t_{1}\bar{t}_{1}\bar{\bar{t}}_{1})\in H,

where
t^{\prime}_{1}=\bar{t}_{1}
or
\bar{\bar{t}}_{1}
. By above claim we have
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t_{1})\in H
, hence
x_{[\beta]}(t_{1})\in H
(by Proposition
5.5.4
). Therefore, we have

x_{1}:=x_{[\alpha]}(t_{2})x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t_{3})x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t_{4})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{5})x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{6})\in H.

Note that

[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{1}]=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t_{2})x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(s_{4})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(s_{5})x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(s_{6})\in H,

for some
s_{4},s_{5},s_{6}\in R
. Again by above claim we have
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm t_{2})\in H
and hence
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{2})\in H
. Thus
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t_{3})x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t_{4})x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{5})x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{6})\in H
. But again by above claim we have
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t_{3}),x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(t_{4}),x_{[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{5}),x_{2[\alpha]+3[\beta]}(t_{6})\in H,
as desired.

Case D. The rank of
\Phi_{\rho}>2
:
Let
[\beta]
be the highest long root in
\Phi_{\rho}
and
[\beta^{\prime}]
be the highest short root in
\Phi_{\rho}
. For
x=\prod_{[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho}}x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
(product is taken over some fixed order on the roots), we set
\Phi(x)=\{[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho}\mid t_{[\alpha]}\neq 0\}
. We use induction on
n
to prove the following statement.

(
P_{n}
):
If
\Phi(x)
only contains the roots
[\beta],[\beta^{\prime}]
or
[\alpha]
with
ht([\alpha])\geq ht([\beta])-n+1
. Then the conclusion of the lemma holds.

Proof of
(P_{1})
: We will show that if
x=x_{[\beta]}(t)x_{[\beta^{\prime}]}(t^{\prime})\in H,
then all factors of
x
is contained in
H
. The subsystem generated by
[\beta]
and
[\beta^{\prime}]
is of the type
{}^{2}A_{3}
if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1}\ (n\geq 3),{}^{2}D_{n}\ (n\geq 4)
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
and is of type
{}^{2}A_{4}
if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n}\ (n\geq 3)
. Thus we are done by Case
A
and Case
B
, above.

Proof of
(P_{n})\implies(P_{n+1})
: Assume that
(P_{n})
holds, that is, assume that if
\Phi(x)
only contains the roots
[\alpha]
with
ht([\alpha])\geq ht([\beta])-n+1
,
[\beta]
or
[\beta^{\prime}]
, then all factors of
x
are contained in
H
. To prove
(P_{n+1})
, let
x\in H
be such that
\Phi(x)
only contains the roots
[\alpha]
with
ht([\alpha])\geq ht([\beta])-n
,
[\beta]
or
[\beta^{\prime}]
. It is enough to show that if
[\delta]\in\Phi(x)
be such that
ht([\delta])=ht([\beta])-n
and
[\delta]\neq[\beta],[\beta^{\prime}]
then
x_{[\delta]}(t_{[\delta]})\in H
. Note that there exists a simple root
[\alpha_{i}]\in\Delta_{\rho}
such that
[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]\in\Phi_{\rho}
and
[\delta]-[\alpha_{i}]\not\in\Phi_{\rho}
(see 3.6 of
[
2
]
). Let
\Phi^{\prime}
be the subsystem generated by
[\alpha_{i}]
and
[\delta]
.

1.

Suppose
\Phi^{\prime}
is of type
A_{2}
. In this case, the pair
[\alpha_{i}]
and
[\delta]
is either of the type
(b-i)
or of the type
(b-ii)
. Take

H\ni[x_{[\alpha_{i}]}(1),x]=\begin{cases}x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t_{[\delta]})x^{\prime}&\text{if }[\alpha_{i}],[\delta]\text{ is of type }(b-i),\\
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t_{[\delta]})x^{\prime}\text{ or }x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm\bar{t}_{[\delta]})x^{\prime}&\text{if }[\alpha_{i}],[\delta]\text{ is of type }(b-ii);\end{cases}

where
x^{\prime}
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\neq[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]
and
ht([\alpha])>ht([\delta])
. Hence, by
(P_{n})
, we have
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t_{[\delta]})
or
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm\bar{t}_{[\delta]})\in H
. But then, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\delta]}(t_{[\delta]})\in H
.

2.

Suppose
\Phi^{\prime}
is of type
B_{2}
and
[\delta]
is a short root. In this case, the pair
[\alpha_{i}]
and
[\delta]
is of the type
(d-i)
if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1},{}^{2}D_{n+1}
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
and of the type
(d-ii)
if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n}
(with
[\alpha_{i}]
being the long root). Take

H\ni[x_{[\alpha_{i}]}(1),x]=\begin{cases}x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(t_{[\delta]})x^{\prime}&\text{if }\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1},{}^{2}D_{n+1}\text{ or }{}^{2}E_{6},\\
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(s_{[\delta]})x^{\prime}&\text{if }\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n};\end{cases}

where
x^{\prime}
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\neq[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]
,
ht([\alpha])>ht([\delta])
and
s_{[\delta]}=(\pm t_{1},t_{2})
or
(\pm\bar{t_{1}},\bar{t_{2}})
if
t_{[\delta]}=(t_{1},t_{2}).
Hence, by
(P_{n})
, we have
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(t_{[\delta]})
or
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(s_{[\delta]})\in H
. But then, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\delta]}(t_{[\delta]})\in H
.

3.

Suppose
\Phi^{\prime}
is of type
B_{2}
and
[\delta_{i}]
is a long root. In this case, the pair
[\delta]
and
[\alpha_{i}]
is of the type
(d-i)
if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n-1},{}^{2}D_{n+1}
or
{}^{2}E_{6}
and of the type
(d-ii)
if
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n}
. Take

H\ni\begin{cases}[x_{[\alpha_{i}]}(1),x]=x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t_{[\delta]})x^{\prime}&\text{if }{}^{2}A_{2n-1},{}^{2}D_{n+1}\text{ or }\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}E_{6},\\
[x_{[\alpha_{i}]}(1,1/2),x]=x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t^{\prime}_{[\delta]},t_{[\delta]}\bar{t}_{[\delta]}/2)x^{\prime}&\text{if }\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n};\end{cases}

where
x^{\prime}
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\neq[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]
,
ht([\alpha])>ht([\delta])
and
t^{\prime}_{[\delta]}=t_{[\delta]}
or
\bar{t}_{[\delta]}
. Hence, by
(P_{n})
, we have
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t_{[\delta]})
or
x_{[\delta]+[\alpha_{i}]}(\pm t^{\prime}_{[\delta]},t_{[\delta]}\bar{t}_{[\delta]}/2)\in H
. But then, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\delta]}(t_{[\delta]})\in H
.

This proves the lemma.
∎

We labelled the simple roots
[\alpha_{1}],[\alpha_{2}],\dots,[\alpha_{l}]
from one end of the Dynkin diagram to the other end such that

[\alpha_{1}]\sim\begin{cases}A_{1}^{2}&\text{if }\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{n}\ (n\geq 3);\\
A_{1}&\text{if }\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}D_{n}\ (n\geq 4),{}^{3}D_{4}\text{ or }{}^{2}E_{6}.\\
\end{cases}

Let
[\beta]
be the highest root in
\Phi_{\rho}
. Note that there is a unique simple root
[\gamma]\in\Delta_{\rho}
such that
\langle[\beta],[\gamma]\rangle\neq 0.

The following table give us the precious values of
[\beta]
and
[\gamma]
:

Type of
\Phi_{\rho}

[\beta]

[\gamma]

{}^{2}A_{2n-1}\ (n\geq 2)

2[\alpha_{1}]+2[\alpha_{2}]+\dots+2[\alpha_{n-1}]+[\alpha_{n}]

[\alpha_{1}]

{}^{2}A_{2n}\ (n\geq 2)

[\alpha_{1}]+2[\alpha_{2}]+2[\alpha_{3}]+\dots+2[\alpha_{n}]

[\alpha_{2}]

{}^{2}D_{n}\ (n\geq 4)

[\alpha_{1}]+2[\alpha_{2}]+2[\alpha_{3}]+\dots+2[\alpha_{n-1}]

[\alpha_{2}]

{}^{3}D_{4}

2[\alpha_{1}]+3[\alpha_{2}]

[\alpha_{1}]

{}^{2}E_{6}

2[\alpha_{1}]+3[\alpha_{2}]+4[\alpha_{3}]+2[\alpha_{4}]

[\alpha_{1}]

Lemma 5.6.2
.

Let
[\beta]
and
[\gamma]
be as above. If
z=x_{[\gamma]}(t)xhy\in U_{\sigma}(R)T_{\sigma}(R)U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)\cap H,
where
x_{[\gamma]}(t)x\in U_{\sigma}(R),

x
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\neq[\gamma],[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho}
and
t_{[\alpha]}\in R_{[\alpha]},h\in T_{\sigma}(R)
and
y\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)
. Then
x_{[\gamma]}(t)\in H
.

Proof.

We write
{}^{a}b
for the conjugate
aba^{-1}
.

Case A. The rank of
\Phi_{\rho}>2
:

Let

\displaystyle H\ni z_{1}

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),z]

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\gamma]}(t)]\{{}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x]\}\{{}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)x}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),h]\}\{{}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)xh}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),y]\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)x}\{\{{}^{x^{-1}x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1}}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\gamma]}(t)]\}\{{}^{x^{-1}}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x]\}\{[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),h]\}\{{}^{h}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),y]\}\}.

Note that,
{}^{x^{-1}x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1}}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\gamma]}(t)]={}^{x^{-1}}[x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)]=[x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)]x^{\prime}
with
x^{\prime}\in U_{\sigma}(R),

[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),h]=x_{-[\gamma]}(a)
for some
a\in R_{[\gamma]}
and
x^{-1}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x]=[x^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)]\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. Set
u_{1}=[x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)],x_{1}=x^{\prime}[x^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)]
and
y_{1}=x_{-[\gamma]}(a)\{{}^{h}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),y]\}
.
Thus we have
z_{1}={}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)x}(u_{1}x_{1}y_{1})
and since
z_{1}\in H
then so is
u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}
.
Observe that
x_{1}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\neq[\gamma]
, and
y_{1}\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)
is a product of elements
x_{-[\alpha]}(s_{[\alpha]})
with
m_{[\gamma]}([\alpha])\geq 1
.
In this case, there exists a root
[\delta]\in\Delta_{\rho}
such that
\{[\gamma],[\delta]\}
is a base of a subsystem of
\Phi_{\rho}
of type
A_{2}
(note that
[\gamma]\sim A_{1}
or
A_{1}^{2}
then so is
[\delta]
). Now let

\displaystyle H\ni z_{2}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}]

\displaystyle=\{[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}]\}\{{}^{u_{1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),x_{1}]\}\{{}^{u_{1}x_{1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),y_{1}]\}

\displaystyle={}^{u_{1}x_{1}}\{\{{}^{x_{1}^{-1}u_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}]\}\{{}^{x_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),x_{1}]\}\{[x_{[\delta]}(1),y_{1}]\}\}.

Note that,

\displaystyle{}^{x_{1}^{-1}u_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}]

\displaystyle={}^{x_{1}^{-1}}[u_{1}^{-1},x_{[\delta]}(1)]

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{-1}(u_{1}^{-1}x_{[\delta]}(1)u_{1}x_{[\delta]}(1)^{-1})x_{1}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{1}^{-1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})\},

where
t^{\prime}=t
or
\bar{t}
. Observe that, there exists
x^{\prime}_{1}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
such that
x_{1}^{-1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)=x_{-[\gamma]}(1)x^{\prime}_{1}
. Then,

\displaystyle z_{2}

\displaystyle={}^{u_{1}x_{1}}\{\{{}^{x_{1}^{-1}u_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}]\}\{{}^{x_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),x_{1}]\}\{[x_{[\delta]}(1),y_{1}]\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{u_{1}x_{1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)}\{\{{}^{x^{\prime}_{1}}(x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2}))\}\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}x_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),x_{1}]\}\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),y_{1}]\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{u_{1}x_{1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)}\{\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x^{\prime\prime}_{1}\}\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}x_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),x_{1}]\}\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),y_{1}]\}\},

where
x^{\prime\prime}_{1}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. Set
x_{2}=x^{\prime\prime}_{1}\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}x_{1}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),x_{1}]\}
and
y_{2}={}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(1),y_{1}]
. Note that
x_{2}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho},[\alpha]\neq[\gamma],[\delta],[\gamma]+[\delta]
and
y_{2}\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)
is a product of elements
x_{-[\alpha]}(s_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho}
and
m_{[\gamma]}([\alpha])\geq 1
. But then

z_{2}=[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}]={}^{u_{1}x_{1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{2}y_{2}\}.

Since
z_{2}\in H
then so is
x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{2}y_{2}
.
Let

\displaystyle H\ni z_{3}

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{2}y_{2}]

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})]\}\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{2}]\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{2}}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),y_{2}]\}

Note that
[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]=1
and
y_{3}:=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),y_{2}]=1
. The formal assertion is clear. To see the latter assertion, observe that the highest root
[\beta]
is the only root with
m_{[\gamma]}([\beta])=2,
therefore
x_{-[\beta]}(u)
is the only possible factor of
y_{3}
.
Assume
y_{3}=x_{-[\beta]}(u)\neq 1,
then
-[\beta]=-[\alpha]-[\gamma]
for some
[\alpha]
such that
x_{-[\alpha]}(v)
is factor of
y_{2}
. Hence,
-[\alpha]=-[\alpha_{1}]+[\delta]
or
(-[\alpha_{1}]+[\delta])-[\alpha_{2}]
for some
[\alpha_{1}],[\alpha_{2}]
with
m_{[\gamma]}([\alpha_{i}])\geq 1\ (i=1,2).
But it is impossible for the first case, as
[\alpha_{1}]=[\beta]-[\gamma]+[\delta]
is never a root, and for the second case,
2\geq m_{[\gamma]}([\alpha_{1}]-[\delta]+[\alpha_{2}])=m_{[\gamma]}([\beta]-[\gamma])=1
. Which proves the claim. Finally, we have

\displaystyle z_{3}

\displaystyle=\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})]\}\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{2}]\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})}\{{}^{x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})^{-1}}[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})]\}\{[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{2}]\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})}\{[x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)][x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{2}]\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{3}\},

where
x_{3}=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),x_{2}]\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. Since
z_{3}\in H
, then so is
x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{3}
. Therefore, by Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})\in H
. But then, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})\in H
. Hence
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})^{-1}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime 2})x_{2}y_{2}\}=x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}y_{2}\in H
. Now let

\displaystyle H\ni z_{4}

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}y_{2}]

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{2}]\}\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]\}

\displaystyle=x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{4}y_{4},

where
x_{4}={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{2}]
and
y_{4}={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]
. Since
x_{2}
does not have factors of the form
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]=[\gamma],[\delta],[\gamma]+[\delta]
, we conclude that
x_{4}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
and it does not have a factor of the form
x_{[\delta]}(s_{[\delta]})
. Now we claim that
[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]=1
or
x_{-[\beta]}(s)
for some
s\in R_{[\beta]}
. The latter case is possible only when
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n+1}
. To see this, observe that the highest root
[\beta]
is the only root with
m_{[\gamma]}([\beta])=2
, therefore
x_{-[\beta]}(u)
is the only possible factor of
[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]
. Assume
\Phi_{\rho}\not\sim{}^{2}A_{2n+1}
and
[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]=x_{-[\beta]}(s)\neq 1
, then
-[\beta]=-[\alpha]-[\gamma]-[\delta]
for some root
[\alpha]
such that
x_{-[\alpha]}(v)
is a factor of
y_{2}
. Hence,
-[\alpha]=-[\alpha_{1}]+[\delta]
or
-[\alpha]=(-[\alpha_{1}]+[\delta])-[\alpha_{2}]
for some
[\alpha_{1}],[\alpha_{2}]
with
m_{[\gamma]}([\alpha_{i}])\geq 1(i=1,2).
But it is impossible for the first case, as in this case
[\alpha_{1}]=[\beta]-2[\gamma]
is never a root, and for the second case,
2\geq m_{[\gamma]}([\alpha_{1}]-[\delta]+[\alpha_{2}])=m_{[\gamma]}([\beta]-[\gamma]-[\delta])=1
. Which proves the claim.

Suppose
\Phi_{\rho}\not\sim{}^{2}A_{2n+1}
then
z_{4}=x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{4}
. In this case, let

\displaystyle H\ni z_{5}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{4}]

\displaystyle=[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{4}]\}

\displaystyle=x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime}).

Thus, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(t)\in H
.

Now suppose
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n+1}
, then
[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]=x_{-[\beta]}(s)
for some
s\in R_{[\beta]}
. We can rewrite the expression of
z_{4}
as follows:

\displaystyle z_{4}

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{2}]\}\{{}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),y_{2}]\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}\{\{{}^{x_{2}^{-1}x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})^{-1}}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\}\{{}^{x_{2}^{-1}}[x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1),x_{2}]\}\{x_{-[\beta]}(s)\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}\{\{{}^{x_{2}^{-1}}[x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1)]\}\{[x_{2}^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1)]\}\{x_{-[\beta]}(s)\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}\{\{{}^{x_{2}^{-1}}x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})\}\{[x_{2}^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1)]\}\{x_{-[\beta]}(s)\}\}

Let
x^{\prime}_{2}
be such that
{}^{x_{2}^{-1}}x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})=x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x^{\prime}_{2}
. Clearly,
x^{\prime}_{2}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. We set
x^{\prime}_{4}=x^{\prime}_{2}[x_{2}^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(1)]
. Note that
x^{\prime}_{4}\in U_{\sigma}(R)
such that it can not contain a factor of type
x_{[\delta]}(s_{[\delta]})
. But then

z_{4}={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{2}}\{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x^{\prime}_{4}x_{-[\beta]}(s)\}.

Since
z_{4}\in H
, so is
x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})^{-1}x^{\prime}_{4}x_{-[\beta]}(s)
. Now, let

\displaystyle H\ni z^{\prime}_{5}

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x^{\prime}_{4}x_{-[\beta]}(s)]

\displaystyle=[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x^{\prime}_{4}]\}\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})x^{\prime}_{4}}[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x_{-[\beta]}(s)]\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})}\{\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})^{-1}}[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\}\{[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x^{\prime}_{4}]\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})}\{\{[x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})^{-1},x_{-[\delta]}(1)]\}\{[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x^{\prime}_{4}]\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})}\{\{x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})\}\{[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x^{\prime}_{4}]\}\}

\displaystyle={}^{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm t^{\prime})}\{x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{5}\},

where
x_{5}=[x_{-[\delta]}(1),x^{\prime}_{4}]\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. Note that
x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{5}\in H
. Finally, let

\displaystyle H\ni z_{6}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{5}]

\displaystyle=[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]\{{}^{x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})}[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{5}]\}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\beta]}(1),x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})x_{[\beta]-2[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(\pm t\bar{t})

Now, by Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\beta]-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime})\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\gamma]}(t)\in H
.

Case B.
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{3}
:

Let
[\delta]\in\Delta_{\rho}
be such that
\{[\gamma],[\delta]\}
forms a base of
\Phi_{\rho}
. Note that
[\gamma]
is a short root. The idea of the proof is the same as in Case A. We leave the details to the reader.
Let
z=x_{[\gamma]}(t)xhy=x_{[\gamma]}(t)xhx_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{1})x_{-[\gamma]}(s_{2})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{3})x_{-[\delta]}(s_{4})\in H
.
We first show that
x_{-[\delta]}(s_{4})\in H
. Write
x^{\prime}=x^{-1}x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1}
and
y^{\prime}=\{x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{1})x_{-[\gamma]}(s_{2})

x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{3})\}^{-1}
and consider

H\ni z_{1}=[x_{[\delta]}(1),z^{-1}]={}^{x_{-[\delta]}(-s_{4})y^{\prime}}\{u_{1}y_{1}x_{1}\},

where
u_{1}=[x_{-[\delta]}(s_{4}),x_{[\delta]}(1)],y_{1}=x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{3})\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)
and
x_{1}=x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{2})x_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{3})\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. Now consider

H\ni z_{2}=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),u_{1}y_{1}x_{1}]={}^{u_{1}y_{1}x_{[\delta]}(1)}\{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s_{4})y_{2}x_{2}\},

where
y_{2}=x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)
and
x_{2}=x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in U_{\sigma}(R)
. Now let
z^{\prime}_{2}=x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s_{4})y_{2}x_{2}
and consider

H\ni z_{3}=[x_{[\delta]}(1),z^{\prime}_{2}]=x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}).

But then

w_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)\{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2})\}w_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2})\in H.

By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime\prime}_{1})\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{1})\in H
. Now
x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-1}(z^{\prime}_{2})=x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s_{4})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in H
. But then

\displaystyle w_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)w_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)\{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s_{4})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})\}w_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)^{-1}w_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm s_{4})x_{[\delta]}(s^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in H.

By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm s_{4})\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{-[\delta]}(s_{4})\in H
.
Finally,
z^{\prime}=zx_{-[\delta]}(s_{4})^{-1}\in H
. Now consider,

H\ni z_{4}=[x_{[\delta]}(1),z^{\prime}]={}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)x}\{x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{4}y_{4}\},

where
x_{4}=x_{[\delta]}(t_{41})x_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t_{42})
and
y_{4}=x_{-[\gamma]}(s_{41})x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{42})
. Finally, we claim that
x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{42})\in H
. To see this consider

H\ni z_{5}=[x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1),x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{4}y_{4}]={}^{x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{4}x_{-[\gamma]}(s_{41})}\{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s_{42})x_{[\delta]}(s_{52})\}.

But then
w_{[\gamma]}(1)\{x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s_{42})x_{[\delta]}(s_{52})\}w_{[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]}(\pm s_{42})x_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm s_{52})\in H.
By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(\pm s_{42})\in H
, and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{-2[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{42})\in H
. Finally, we have
z^{\prime\prime}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{4}y_{4}x_{[\gamma]}(\pm s_{42})^{-1}\in H
. But then

w_{[\gamma]}(1)z^{\prime\prime}w_{[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{2[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t_{41})x_{[\delta]}(\pm t_{42})x_{[\gamma]}(\pm s_{41})\in H.

By Lemma
5.6.1
,
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t)\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\gamma]}(t)\in H
.

Case C.
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{4}
:

Let
[\delta]\in\Delta_{\rho}
be such that
\{[\gamma],[\delta]\}
forms a base of
\Phi_{\rho}
. Note that
[\gamma]
is a long root. The idea of the proof is the same as in Case A. We leave the details to the reader.
We first consider

z_{1}=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),z]={}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)x}(u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}),

where
u_{1}=[x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)],

x_{1}=x_{[\delta]}(t_{1})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t_{2})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t_{3})
and

y_{1}=x_{-[\gamma]}(s_{1})

x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{2})

x_{-[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(s_{3})
.
We than consider

\displaystyle H\ni z_{2}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\delta]}(1,1/2),u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}]

\displaystyle={}^{u_{1}x_{1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t,(t\bar{t}\pm(t-\bar{t}))/2)x_{2}y_{2}\},

where
x_{2}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{2})
and
y_{2}=x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})
. Set
u_{2}=x_{[\delta]}(\pm t,(t\bar{t}\pm(t-\bar{t}))/2)
and
z^{\prime}_{2}=u_{2}x_{2}y_{2}
.
Our next goal is to show that
x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})\in H
. Write
s^{\prime}_{2}=(a,b)\in R_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}
. Now consider

\displaystyle H\ni z_{3}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\delta]}(1,1/2),z^{\prime}_{2}]={}^{u_{2}x_{2}}\{x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm a^{\prime})\},

where
a^{\prime}=a
or
\bar{a}
and
t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\in R_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}
. Since
z_{3}\in H
we have
x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm a^{\prime})\in H
. But then
w_{[\gamma]}(1)\{x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm a^{\prime})\}w_{[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]}(\pm a^{\prime})\in H
(see Proposition
4.4.10
). By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(\pm a^{\prime})\in H
. Next, we consider

\displaystyle H\ni z_{4}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(1),z^{\prime}_{2}]={}^{u_{2}x_{2}}\{x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm b^{\prime})\},

where
b^{\prime}=b
or
\bar{b}
and
t^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in R_{[\delta]}
. Since
z_{3}\in H
we have
x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm b^{\prime})\in H
. But then
w_{[\gamma]}(1)\{x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm b^{\prime})\}w_{[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{[\gamma]}(\pm b^{\prime})\in H
(see Proposition
4.4.10
). By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(\pm b^{\prime})\in H
. Since
x_{[\gamma]}(\pm a^{\prime})\in H
and
x_{[\gamma]}(\pm b^{\prime})\in H
, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(a,b)\in H
. But then

z_{2}x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(a,b)^{-1}=x_{[\delta]}(\pm t,t\bar{t}\pm(t-\bar{t}))/2)x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{2})x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})\in H.

Finally,

\displaystyle w_{[\gamma]}(1)\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t,t\bar{t}\pm(t-\bar{t}))/2)x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{2})x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})\}w_{[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime},t\bar{t}\pm(t-\bar{t}))/2)x_{[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{11})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{22})x_{[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{11})\in H,

where
t^{\prime}=t
or
\bar{t}
, and
t^{\prime}_{11}\in R_{[\delta]},t^{\prime}_{22}\in R_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]},s^{\prime}_{11}\in R_{[\gamma]}
. Finally, by Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime},t\bar{t}\pm(t-\bar{t}))/2)\in H
, and by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(t)\in H
, as desired.

Case D.
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{3}D_{4}
:
Let
[\delta]\in\Delta_{\rho}
be such that
\{[\gamma],[\delta]\}
forms a base of
\Phi_{\rho}
. Note that
[\gamma]
is a long root. The idea of the proof is the same as in Case A. We leave the details to the reader.
We first consider

z_{1}=[x_{-[\gamma]}(1),z]={}^{x_{[\gamma]}(t)x}(u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}),

where
u_{1}=[x_{[\gamma]}(t)^{-1},x_{-[\gamma]}(1)],x_{1}=x_{[\delta]}(t_{1})x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t_{2})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t_{3})x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t_{4})x_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t_{5})
and

y_{1}=x_{-[\gamma]}(s_{1})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s_{2})x_{-[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(s_{3})x_{-[\gamma]-3[\delta]}(s_{4})x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\delta]}(s_{5})
.
We than consider

\displaystyle H\ni z_{2}=[x_{[\delta]}(1),u_{1}x_{1}y_{1}]={}^{u_{1}x_{1}x_{-[\gamma]}(1)}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{2}y_{2}\},

where
x_{2}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{2})x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{3})x_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{4})
and
y_{2}=x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})

x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})

x_{-[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{3})x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{4})
. We set
z^{\prime}_{2}=x_{[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{2}y_{2}
.
Our next goal is to show that
x_{-[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{3})\in H
. Now consider

\displaystyle H\ni z_{3}

\displaystyle=[x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(1),z^{\prime}_{2}]

\displaystyle={}^{x_{[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{2}x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{4})}\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{4}\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055ptx_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}})x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\beta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})\}.

Since
z_{3}\in H
we have

x_{[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{4}\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}})x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\beta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})\in H.

But then

\displaystyle w_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(1)\{x_{[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3})x_{-[\gamma]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{4}\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}})x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\beta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})\}w_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(1)^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3})x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{4}\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}})x_{2[\gamma]+3[\beta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})\in H.

By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}),x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{4}\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{-[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{3}),x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(\pm s^{\prime}_{3}\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}\bar{\bar{s^{\prime}_{3}}})\in H
. But then
x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{4})\in H
, and again by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{4})\in H
. Therefore

\displaystyle z^{\prime\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle=z^{\prime}_{2}x_{-2[\gamma]-3[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{4})^{-1}x_{-[\gamma]-2[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{3})^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{2})x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{3})x_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{4})x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})\in H.

Now replacing
z^{\prime}_{2}
by
w_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(1)z^{\prime}_{2}w_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(1)^{-1}
in above process we can conclude that
x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})\in H
. Therefore, we have

z^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}=x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(t^{\prime}_{1})^{-1}z^{\prime\prime}_{2}=x_{[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{3})x_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{4})x_{-[\gamma]}(s^{\prime}_{1})x_{-[\gamma]-[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})\in H.

Finally,

\displaystyle z_{4}

\displaystyle=w_{[\gamma]}(1)w_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)\{z^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}\}w_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(1)^{-1}w_{[\gamma]}(1)^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(\pm t)x_{[\gamma]+[\delta]}(\pm t^{\prime\prime}_{2})x_{2[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{3})x_{[\gamma]}(t^{\prime\prime}_{4})x_{[\gamma]+3[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{1})x_{[\delta]}(s^{\prime}_{2})\in H,

Thus, by Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\gamma]+2[\delta]}(\pm t)\in H
, and by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\gamma]}(t)\in H
, as desired.
∎

Remark
.

The idea of the above proof is motivated by that of the Lemma in 3.4 of
[
58
]
. However, that proof contains an error, which we rectify here. Specifically, the value of
s
on page 11 is incorrect; the correct value should be
s=\pm a_{1}at^{2}
instead of
s=\pm a_{1}t+a_{1}at^{2}
. This leads to a mistake in the last paragraph of the proof of Case 1, where
x_{\beta}(\pm a_{1}at^{2})\in H
should appear instead of
x_{\beta}(\pm a_{1}t\pm a_{1}at^{2})\in H
. However, the corrected statement does not yield the required result.

Proof of Proposition
5.4.2
.
The first part is clear from Lemma
5.6.1
. For the second part, let
xhy\in U_{\sigma}(rad(R))T_{\sigma}(R)U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)\cap H
, where
x\in U_{\sigma}(rad(R)),h\in T_{\sigma}(R)
and
y\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)
.

First assume that
x=1=y
. Then
z=h=h(\chi)\in T_{\sigma}(R)
for some
\chi
. Since
h(\chi)\in H
, for any root
\alpha\in\Phi
we have
[x_{[\alpha]}(1),h(\chi)]=x_{[\alpha]}(1-\chi(\alpha))
is an element of
H
. Thus,
1-\chi(\alpha)\in J
, that is,
\chi(\alpha)\equiv 1
(mod
J
) for every
\alpha\in\Phi
. Therefore, by Lemma
5.1.6
, we have
h(\chi)\in T_{\sigma}(R,J)
.

We now assume that
x\neq 1
or
y\neq 1
. Observe that, if we prove that every factor of
x
and
y
is in
H
then we are done. Furthermore, by conjugating with elements of the Weyl group, applying Lemma
5.1.2
, and using the Chevalley commutator formulas, it suffices to prove the following:

for a fixed short root (respectively, a fixed long root)
[\alpha]
, any factor of the form
x_{[\alpha]}(t)
appearing in the expression of an element
x^{\prime}
or
y^{\prime}
, where
z^{\prime}=x^{\prime}h^{\prime}y^{\prime}\in U_{\sigma}(R)\,T_{\sigma}(R)\,U^{-}_{\sigma}(R)\cap H
, is contained in
H
.

By the Lemma
5.6.2
and the above observation, if
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
is a factor of
x
or
y
with
[\alpha]
being the same type as
[\gamma]
then
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})\in H
. Now it remains to show that
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})\in H
for a root
[\alpha]
not of the type
[\gamma]
. Note that we can assume that both
x
and
y
contains only factors
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
where
[\alpha]
is not of the type
[\gamma]
.

Suppose
\Phi_{\rho}\not\sim{}^{2}A_{2n+1}
, then
[\gamma]
is a long root and hence it is of the same type as the highest root
[\beta]
. In particular, by our assumption,
x
and
y
does not contains a factor
x_{[\beta]}(t_{[\beta]})
and
x_{-[\beta]}(t_{[\beta]})
, respectively. Let
x_{[\alpha_{1}]}(t)
be a factor of
x
or
y
. Then
[\alpha_{1}]
is of different type then
[\gamma]
. Choose a root
[\alpha_{2}]\in\Phi_{\rho}^{+}
such that
-[\alpha_{2}]
and
[\beta]
generates the subsystem of type
B_{2}
. Since
[\alpha_{1}]
and
[\alpha_{2}]
is of the same type, we can conjugate
z
by an element of the Weyl group in such a way that
x_{-[\alpha_{2}]}(t)
is a factor of that new element, say
z_{1}
. Now let
z_{2}=[x_{[\beta]}(1),z_{1}]
. Then
z_{2}\in U_{\sigma}(R)\cap H
and it contains a factor
x_{[\beta]-[\alpha_{2}]}(t^{\prime})
, where
t^{\prime}=t
or
\bar{t}
if
[\alpha_{2}]\not\sim A_{2}
; and
t^{\prime}=(t_{1},t_{2}),(t_{1},\bar{t}_{2})
or
(\bar{t}_{1},t_{2})
if
[\alpha_{2}]\sim A_{2}
and
t=(t_{1},t_{2})
. By Lemma
5.6.1
, we have
x_{[\beta]-[\alpha_{2}]}(t^{\prime})\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\alpha_{1}]}(t)\in H
.

Now suppose
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{2n+1}
, then
[\gamma]
is a short root. Let
x_{[\alpha_{1}]}(t)
be a factor of
x
or
y
with
[\alpha_{1}]
being a long root. Choose a root
[\alpha_{2}]\in\Phi_{\rho}
such that
[\alpha_{1}]
and
[\alpha_{2}]
form a subsystem of type
B_{2}
. Let
z_{1}=[x_{[\alpha_{2}]}(1),z]
. Then
z_{1}\in H
contains a factor
x_{[\alpha_{1}]+[\alpha_{2}]}(t)
with
[\alpha_{1}]+[\alpha_{2}]
being a short root, that is, it is of the same type as
[\gamma]
. Hence from above observation,
x_{[\alpha_{1}]+[\alpha_{2}]}(t)\in H
and hence, by Proposition
5.5.4
,
x_{[\alpha]}(t)\in H
, as desired.
∎

5.7
Proof of Proposition
5.4.3

Let the notations be as in Section
5.4
. Define
k_{\mathfrak{m}}=R/\mathfrak{m}\cong R_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}R_{\mathfrak{m}}
, and similarly for
k_{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}
and
k_{\bar{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}}
. Clearly,
R_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong R_{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}\cong R_{\bar{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}}
and
k_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong k_{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}\cong k_{\bar{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}}
. For
S=S_{\mathfrak{m}}
, we set

I_{S}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{m}&\text{if }\mathfrak{m}=\bar{\mathfrak{m}},\\
\mathfrak{m}\cap\bar{\mathfrak{m}}&\text{if }\mathfrak{m}\neq\bar{\mathfrak{m}}\text{ and }o(\theta)=2,\\
\mathfrak{m}\cap\bar{\mathfrak{m}}\cap\bar{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}&\text{if }\mathfrak{m}\neq\bar{\mathfrak{m}}\text{ and }o(\theta)=3;\end{cases}\text{ and }k_{S}=\begin{cases}k_{\mathfrak{m}}&\text{if }\mathfrak{m}=\bar{\mathfrak{m}},\\
k_{\mathfrak{m}}\times k_{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}&\text{if }\mathfrak{m}\neq\bar{\mathfrak{m}}\text{ and }o(\theta)=2,\\
k_{\mathfrak{m}}\times k_{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}\times k_{\bar{\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}}&\text{if }\mathfrak{m}\neq\bar{\mathfrak{m}}\text{ and }o(\theta)=3.\end{cases}

Then
R/I_{S}\cong R_{S}/(I_{S}R_{S})\cong k_{S}
.
Note that the ring automorphism
\theta:R\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow R
also induces an automorphism of
k_{S}
, which is also denoted by
\theta
.

Proposition 5.7.1
.

Let the notations be as in Section
5.4
.

1.

U_{\sigma}(R_{S})\cap\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)\subset U_{\sigma}(J_{S})
.

2.

U_{\sigma}(I_{S}R_{S})T_{\sigma}(R_{S})U^{-}_{\sigma}(R_{S})\cap\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)\subset U_{\sigma}(J_{S})T_{\sigma}(R_{S},J_{S})U^{-}_{\sigma}(J_{S}).

At first glance, the above proposition may appear to be an immediate consequence of Proposition
5.4.2
, but this is not the case. The crucial point is that the subgroup
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)
of
G_{\sigma}(R_{S})
may not be normalized by
E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(R_{S})
. However, once we prove the following lemma, the proof of the above lemma will be similar to the proof of Proposition
5.4.2
and hence is omitted.

Lemma 5.7.2
.

Let the notations be as in Section
5.4
. Let
z\in H
.

1.

If
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)=\prod_{[\alpha]\in\Phi_{\rho}^{+}}x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})\in U_{\sigma}(R_{S})
with
t_{[\alpha]}\in(R_{S})_{[\alpha]}
, then for each
[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho}
there exists
s_{[\alpha]}\in S_{\theta}
such that
x_{[\alpha]}(s_{[\alpha]}\cdot t_{[\alpha]})\in H
.

2.

Let
[\beta]
and
[\gamma]
be as in Lemma
5.6.2
. If
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)=x_{[\gamma]}(t)xhy\in U_{\sigma}(R_{S})T_{\sigma}(R_{S})U^{-}_{\sigma}(R_{S}),
where
x_{[\gamma]}(t)x\in U_{\sigma}(R_{S}),

x
is a product of elements
x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})
with
[\alpha]\neq[\gamma],[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}_{\rho}
and
t_{[\alpha]}\in(R_{S})_{[\alpha]},h\in T_{\sigma}(R_{S})
and
y\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(R_{S})
. Then there exists
s\in S_{\theta}
such that
x_{[\gamma]}(s\cdot t)\in H
.

Proof.

The proofs of parts (a) and (b) follow a similar approach to Lemmas
5.6.1
and
5.6.2
, respectively, but also incorporate the method used in proving Proposition
5.4.6
.
Let
\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}
be as in the proof of Proposition
5.4.6
.

(a) Let
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{[\alpha]\in\Phi^{+}}x_{[\alpha]}(t_{[\alpha]})\in U_{\sigma}(R_{S})\subset U_{\sigma}(R_{S}[X])
, with
t_{[\alpha]}\in(R_{S})_{[\alpha]}\subset(R_{S}[X])_{[\alpha]}
. We will first prove the result in the case where
\Phi_{\rho}\sim{}^{2}A_{3}
. The other cases can be proven similarly using the same techniques (cf. Lemma
5.6.1
) and are therefore omitted.

As in Lemma
5.6.1
, let
[\alpha]
and
[\beta]
be the simple roots, with
[\alpha]
being the long root. We first claim that

\begin{split}\text{if }\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(u)\in\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H),\text{ then }\\
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(s_{1}\cdot t),x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(s_{2}\cdot u)\in H\text{ for some }s_{1},s_{2}\in S_{\theta}.\end{split}

(5.8)

Since
\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}\mid_{G_{\sigma}(R_{S})}=\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}
, we have
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)=\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)\in\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)
. For any
r\in(R_{S}[X])_{[\alpha]}=(R_{S}[X])_{\theta}
, we have

[x_{-[\alpha]}(r),\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)]=x_{[\beta]}(\pm rt)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm rt\bar{t}).

Write
t=a/b
where
a\in R
and
b\in S
. Let
r=b\bar{b}r^{\prime}X
with
r^{\prime}\in S_{\theta}
. Then

\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}([x_{-[\alpha]}(b\bar{b}r^{\prime}X),z])=\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}r^{\prime}tX)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}r^{\prime}t\bar{t}X)).

Now let

\epsilon(X)=[x_{-[\alpha]}(b\bar{b}r^{\prime}X),z](x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}r^{\prime}tX)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}r^{\prime}t\bar{t}X))^{-1}.

Then
\epsilon(X)
satisfies the hypothesis of Lemma
5.4.5
, and hence there exists
s^{\prime}\in S_{\theta}
such that
\epsilon(s^{\prime}X)=1
. Thus we have

[x_{-[\alpha]}(b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}X),z]=x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}tX)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t\bar{t}X).

Setting
X=1
, we get

[x_{-[\alpha]}(b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}),z]=x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t\bar{t})\in H.

Since
\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)\in\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)
, then so is
(\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{m}}(z))^{-1}=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(-t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(-u)
. Therefore, we can replace
t
and
u
by
-t
and
-u
respectively, and we obtain

x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}(-t))x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t\bar{t})\in H.

Thus,

\begin{split}x_{[\beta]}(\pm 2b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t)=\left\{x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t\bar{t})\right\}\\
\left\{x_{[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}(-t))x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm b\bar{b}s^{\prime}r^{\prime}t\bar{t})\right\}^{-1}\in H.\end{split}

Set
r^{\prime}=\pm 1/2
and let
s_{1}=b\bar{b}s^{\prime}\in S_{\theta}
, then
x_{[\beta]}(s_{1}t)\in H
. By Proposition
5.5.4
, we get
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(s_{1}t)\in H
.
Now, in
G_{\sigma}(R_{S})
, for any
r_{1}\in R
, we have

[x_{-[\beta]}(r_{1}),\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)]=x_{[\alpha]}(\pm(r_{1}\bar{t}+\bar{r}_{1}t))x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm r_{1}u)x_{[\alpha]}(\pm r_{1}\bar{r}_{1}u)\in\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H).

Put
r_{1}=s_{1}
, then by the proof of Lemma
5.5.5
, we have
x_{[\alpha]}(\pm(s_{1}\bar{t}+\bar{s}_{1}t))\in\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)
, and hence

\begin{split}x_{[\alpha]}(\pm(s_{1}\bar{t}+\bar{s}_{1}t))^{-1}(x_{[\alpha]}(\pm(s_{1}\bar{t}+\bar{s}_{1}t))x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm s_{1}u)x_{[\alpha]}(\pm s_{1}\bar{s}_{1}u))\\
=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm s_{1}u)x_{[\alpha]}(\pm s_{1}\bar{s}_{1}u)\in\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H).\end{split}

Thus,

w_{[\beta]}(1)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm s_{1}u)x_{[\alpha]}(\pm s_{1}\bar{s}_{1}u)w_{[\beta]}(1)^{-1}=x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(\pm s_{1}u)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(\pm s_{1}\bar{s}_{1}u)\in\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H).

Finally, by the above argument, we have
x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(s_{2}u)\in H
for some
s_{2}\in S_{\theta}
. Therefore, by Proposition
5.5.4
, we have
x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(s_{2}u)\in H
. This proves (
5.8
).

Now let
x=x_{[\beta]}(t)x_{[\alpha]}(u)x_{[\alpha]+[\beta]}(v)x_{[\alpha]+2[\beta]}(w)\in H
. By a similar argument as above and as in Lemma
5.6.1
, we can prove the desired result.

(b) This part can also be proven using the same reasoning as in the proofs of Lemma
5.6.2
and the methods of G. Taddei
[
59
]
(cf. Lemma
5.4.5
), similar to the approach taken for part (a). Therefore, we omit the detailed proof.
∎

Remark
.

To prove Proposition
5.7.1
, we must use not only the method of proof of Proposition
5.4.2
, but also the lemma by G. Taddei
[
59
, Lemma 3.14]
, as we did in the proof of the previous lemma.

Proof of Proposition
5.4.3
.
If
J_{S}=R_{S}
, the proof is complete. Therefore, we assume
J_{S}\neq R_{S}
. Since
J_{S}
is a
\theta
-invariant proper ideal of
R_{S}
, we have
J_{S}\subset\operatorname{rad}(R_{S})=I_{S}R_{S}
. By Corollary
5.1.9
, it follows that

G_{\sigma}(R_{S},J_{S})=U_{\sigma}(J_{S})T_{\sigma}(R_{S},J_{S})U^{-}_{\sigma}(J_{S}).

Assume, for the sake of contradiction, that
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)\not\subset G_{\sigma}(R_{S},J_{S})
. Under this assumption, we will show that there exists an element
z\in H
such that
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)\not\in G_{\sigma}(R_{S},J_{S})
and
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)\in U_{\sigma}(I_{S}R_{S})T_{\sigma}(R_{S})U^{-}_{\sigma}(R_{S})
. This, however, leads to a contradiction with Proposition
5.7.1
.

Let
\pi:G_{\sigma}(R_{S})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow G_{\sigma}(k_{S})
be the canonical homomorphism.
Suppose
\pi(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H))
is central. Then
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H)\subset G_{\sigma}(R_{S},I_{S}R_{S})
and hence, by Corollary
5.1.9
, we are done.
Now assume that
\pi(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H))
is non-central. Since
\pi\circ\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}
is surjective on elementary subgroups, the subgroup
\pi(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H))
of
G_{\sigma}(k_{S})
is normalized by
E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(k_{S})
. We claim that
E^{\prime}_{\sigma}(k_{S})\subset\pi(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(H))
. Assuming the claim to be true for the moment, let us proceed to prove the rest of the result.
For a given
x_{[\alpha]}(t+I_{S})\in U^{-}_{\sigma}(k_{S})\ (t\not\in I_{S})
, by our claim, there exists
z\in H
such that

\pi(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z))=x_{[\alpha]}(t+I_{S})=\pi(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(x_{[\alpha]}(t))).

Therefore,
\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)(\psi_{\mathfrak{m}}(x_{[\alpha]}(t)))^{-1}\in\ker{\pi}=G_{\sigma}(I_{S}R_{S})=U_{\sigma}(I_{S}R_{S})T_{\sigma}(I_{S}R_{S})U^{-}_{\sigma}(I_{S}R_{S})
